**Ronald Wilson Reagan**[^cite_note-8] (February 6, 1911 – June 5, 2004) was an American politician and actor who served as the 40th [president of the United States](24113.md) from 1981 to 1989. A member of the [Republican Party](32070.md), he became an important figure in the [American conservative movement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_conservative_movement). The period encompassing [his presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Ronald_Reagan) is known as the [Reagan era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_era).

Born in [Tampico, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampico,_Illinois), Reagan graduated from [Eureka College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eureka_College) in 1932 and was hired the next year as a sports broadcaster in Iowa. In 1937, he moved to California where he became a well-known film actor. During his acting career, Reagan was president of the [Screen Actors Guild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Screen_Actors_Guild) twice from 1947 to 1952 and from 1959 to 1960. In the 1950s, he hosted *[General Electric Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric_Theater)* and worked as a motivational speaker for [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric). During the [1964 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_United_States_presidential_election), Reagan's "[A Time for Choosing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Time_for_Choosing)" speech launched his rise as a leading conservative figure. After being [elected governor of California in 1966](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_California_gubernatorial_election), he raised state taxes, turned the state budget deficit into a surplus, and implemented crackdowns on university protests. Following his loss to [Gerald Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Ford) in the [1976 Republican Party presidential primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries), Reagan won the Republican Party's nomination and then obtained a landslide victory over President [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter) in the [1980 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_presidential_election).

In his first term as president, Reagan began implementing "[Reaganomics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reaganomics)", a policy involving economic deregulation and cuts to both taxes and government spending during [a period of stagflation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_1980s_recession_in_the_United_States). On the world stage, he [escalated the arms race](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strategic_Defense_Initiative), increased military spending, [shifted Cold War policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_Doctrine) away from *[détente](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%C3%A9tente)*, and ordered the [1983 invasion of Grenada](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1983_invasion_of_Grenada). Other notable events during Reagan's first term included [his survival of an assassination attempt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attempted_assassination_of_Ronald_Reagan), a well-publicized fight with public-sector labor unions, an expansion of the [war on drugs](1181646.md), and [his administration's slow response to the AIDS epidemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_and_AIDS). In the [1984 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_United_States_presidential_election), he was elected to a second term by defeating former vice president [Walter Mondale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walter_Mondale) in [one of the largest landslide victories in American history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_presidential_elections_by_popular_vote_margin). Foreign affairs dominated his second term, including the [1986 bombing of Libya](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1986_bombing_of_Libya), the [secret and illegal sale of arms to Iran to fund the Contras](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran%E2%80%93Contra_affair), and negotiations with Soviet leader [Mikhail Gorbachev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikhail_Gorbachev) that culminated in the [Intermediate-Range Nuclear Forces Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intermediate-Range_Nuclear_Forces_Treaty).

Reagan left the presidency in 1989 with the American economy having experienced a significant reduction of inflation, a decline in the unemployment rate, and the longest peacetime economic expansion in US history at that time. The national debt had nearly tripled since 1981 as a result of his tax cuts and increased military spending outweighing his cuts to domestic discretionary spending. [Reagan's foreign policies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan's_foreign_policies) also contributed to the end of the Cold War. Although he planned an active post-presidency, those plans were hindered after he was diagnosed with [Alzheimer's disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alzheimer's_disease) in 1994. His physical and mental capacities gradually deteriorated leading to [his death](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_and_state_funeral_of_Ronald_Reagan) in 2004. His tenure constituted a [realignment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realigning_election) toward conservative policies in the United States, and he is often considered an icon of American conservatism. [Historical rankings of US presidents](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_rankings_of_US_presidents) have typically placed Reagan in the middle to upper tier, and [his post-presidential approval ratings by the general public](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_on_the_Ronald_Reagan_administration) have generally remained high.

| [v](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Ronald_Reagan_series)[t](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template_talk\:Ronald_Reagan_series)[e](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special\:EditPage/Template\:Ronald_Reagan_series) |

Ronald Wilson Reagan was born on February 6, 1911, in [an apartment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Birthplace_of_Ronald_Reagan) in [Tampico, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tampico,_Illinois), the younger son of [Nelle Clyde Wilson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nelle_Wilson_Reagan) and [Jack Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jack_Reagan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor20045-9] Nelle was committed to the [Disciples of Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disciples_of_Christ),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200412-10] which believed in the [Social Gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Gospel).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESpitz201836-11] She led [prayer meetings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prayer_meeting) and ran mid-week prayers at her church when the pastor was out of town.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200412-10] Reagan credited her spiritual influence[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200448-12] and he became a [Christian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christianity_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200410-13] According to American political figure [Stephen Vaughn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_Vaughn), Reagan's values came from his pastor, and the First Christian Church's religious, economic and social positions "coincided with the words, if not the beliefs of the latter-day Reagan".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1995109-14] Jack focused on making money to take care of the family,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor20045-9] but this was complicated by his alcoholism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201510-15] Reagan had an older brother, [Neil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neil_Reagan).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor20044-16] The family lived in Chicago, [Galesburg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Galesburg,_Illinois), and [Monmouth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monmouth,_Illinois) before returning to Tampico. In 1920, they settled in [Dixon, Illinois](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dixon,_Illinois),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton19985-17] living in [a house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_Boyhood_Home) near the [H. C. Pitney Variety Store Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/H._C._Pitney_Variety_Store_Building).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard20124-18] Reagan attended [Dixon High School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dixon_High_School_\(Illinois\)), where he developed interests in drama and [football](18951490.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201514-19] His first job was as a [lifeguard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lifeguard) at the [Rock River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_River_\(Mississippi_River_tributary\)) in [Lowell Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lowell_Park_\(Dixon,_Illinois\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201516-20]

In 1928, Reagan began attending [Eureka College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eureka_College),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199810-21] which Nelle approved because of its affiliation with the Disciples of Christ.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201517-22] He was a mediocre student[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201521-23] who participated in [cheerleading](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheerleading),[^cite_note-24] sports, drama, and campus politics. He became [student body president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Student_body_president) and joined a student strike that resulted in the college president's resignation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199810–11-25] Reagan was initiated as a member of [Tau Kappa Epsilon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tau_Kappa_Epsilon) fraternity and served as president of the local chapter.[^cite_note-26] Reagan played at the [guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guard_\(American_football\)) position for the [1930](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1930_Eureka_Red_Devils_football_team) and [1931 Eureka Red Devils football teams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1931_Eureka_Red_Devils_football_team) and recalled a time when two Black teammates were refused service at a [segregated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States) hotel; he invited them to his parents' home nearby in Dixon and his parents welcomed them. At the time, his parents' stance on racial questions was unusually [progressive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressivism_in_the_United_States) in Dixon.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2000457Mayer201573-27] Reagan himself had grown up with very few [Black Americans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Americans) and felt oblivious to racial discrimination.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrimuth201642-28]

## Entertainment career

Further information: [Ronald Reagan filmography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_filmography)

### Radio and film

A frame of Ronald Reagan in the 1939 film Dark Victory

*[Dark Victory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_Victory)* (1939)

A frame of Reagan in the 1941 film The Bad Man

*[The Bad Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Bad_Man_\(1941_film\))* (1941)

After obtaining a [Bachelor of Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bachelor_of_Arts) in economics and sociology from [Eureka College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eureka_College) in 1932,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMullen1999207-29][^cite_note-30] Reagan took a job in [Davenport, Iowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Davenport,_Iowa), as a sports broadcaster for four football games in the [Big Ten Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Conference).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201524–26-31] He then worked for [WHO radio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WHO_\(AM\)) in [Des Moines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Des_Moines) as a broadcaster for the [Chicago Cubs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chicago_Cubs). His specialty was creating play-by-play accounts of games using only basic descriptions that the station received by wire as the games were in progress.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201529–30-32] Simultaneously, he often expressed his opposition to racism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2000458-33] In 1936, while traveling with the Cubs to their spring training in California, Reagan took a screen test that led to a seven-year contract with [Warner Bros. Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros._Pictures).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201218–19-34]

Reagan arrived in [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles) in 1937, debuting in *[Love Is on the Air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_Is_on_the_Air)* (1937).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201539–40-35] Using a simple and direct approach to acting and following his directors' instructions,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFreie201543–44-36] he made thirty films, mostly [B films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B_films), before beginning [military service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_history_of_the_United_States_during_World_War_II) in April 1942.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn199430-37] He broke out of these types of films by portraying [George Gipp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Gipp) in *[Knute Rockne, All American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knute_Rockne,_All_American)* (1940), which would be rejuvenated when reporters called Reagan "the Gipper" while he campaigned for president.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200113–15-38] Reagan starred in *[Kings Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kings_Row)* (1942) as a leg amputee;[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201225–26-39] this performance was considered his best by many critics.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn199437-40] Reagan became a star,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFriedrich199789-41] with [Gallup polls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallup_polls) placing him "in the top 100 stars" from 1941 to 1942.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn199437-40]

[World War II](32927.md) interrupted the movie stardom that Reagan would never be able to achieve again[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEFriedrich199789-41] as Warner Bros. became uncertain about his ability to generate ticket sales. Reagan, who had a limited acting range, was dissatisfied with the roles he received. [Lew Wasserman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lew_Wasserman) renegotiated his contract with his studio, allowing him to also make films with [Universal Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures), [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures), and [RKO Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/RKO_Pictures) as a freelancer. Reagan appeared in multiple [western films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_film), something that had been denied to him while working at Warner Bros.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200359-42] In 1952, he ended his relationship with Warner Bros.,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994236-43] but went on to appear in a total of 53 films,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn199430-37] his last being *[The Killers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Killers_\(1964_film\))* (1964).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994312-44]

### Military service

Captain Reagan in the Army Air Force working for the 1st Motion Picture Unit in Culver City, California, between 1943 and 1944

Reagan at [Fort Roach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Motion_Picture_Unit#Life_at_Fort_Roach), between 1943 and 1944

In April 1937, Reagan enlisted in the [United States Army Reserve](1187028.md). He was assigned as a [private](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Private_\(rank\)) in Des Moines' [322nd Cavalry Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/322nd_Cavalry_Regiment_\(United_States\)) and reassigned to [second lieutenant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_lieutenant) in the Officers Reserve Corps.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliverMarion2010148-45] He later became a part of the [323rd Cavalry Regiment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/323rd_Cavalry_Regiment_\(United_States\)) in California.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn199496-46] As relations between the United States and [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) worsened, Reagan was ordered for active duty while he was filming *[Kings Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kings_Row)*. Wasserman and Warner Bros. lawyers successfully sent draft deferments to complete the film in October 1941. However, to avoid accusations of Reagan being a [draft dodger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Draft_dodger), the studio let him go in April 1942.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201226Brands201554–55-47]

Reagan reported for duty with severe [near-sightedness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Near-sightedness). His first assignment was at [Fort Mason](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_Mason) as a [liaison officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liaison_officer), a role that allowed him to transfer to the [United States Army Air Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Air_Forces) (AAF). Reagan became an AAF [public relations officer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_relations_officer) and was assigned to the [18th AAF Base Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/18th_AAF_Base_Unit) in [Culver City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culver_City)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliverMarion2010148–149-48] where he felt that it was "impossible to remove an incompetent or lazy worker" due to what he felt was "the incompetence, the delays, and inefficiencies" of the federal [bureaucracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bureaucracy).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201227-49] Despite this, Reagan participated in the Provisional Task Force Show Unit in [Burbank](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burbank,_California)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliverMarion2010149-50] and continued to make theatrical films.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201557-51] He was also ordered to temporary duty in New York City to participate in the [sixth War Loan Drive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Series_E_bond) before being reassigned to [Fort MacArthur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fort_MacArthur) until his discharge on December 9, 1945, as a [captain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Captain_\(United_States_O-3\)). Throughout his military service, Reagan produced over 400 [training films](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Training_film).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOliverMarion2010149-50]

### Screen Actors Guild presidency



Seven actors and studio workers during a telephone conference held in the aftermath of [Hollywood Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Black_Friday) in which [American Federation of Labor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Federation_of_Labor) officials denied issuing a "clarification" which set off the film strike, October 26, 1946.\
**(L-R):** James Skelton, [Herbert Sorrell](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Herbert_Sorrell), **Ronald Reagan**, [Edward Arnold](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Edward_Arnold_\(actor\)), Roy Tindall, [George Murphy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Murphy), and [Gene Kelly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_Kelly).

When [Robert Montgomery](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Robert_Montgomery_\(actor\)) resigned as president of the [Screen Actors Guild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Screen_Actors_Guild) (SAG) on March 10, 1947, Reagan was elected to that position in a special election.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200386-52] Reagan's first tenure saw various labor–management disputes,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994133-53] the [Hollywood blacklist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_blacklist),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994146-54] and the [Taft–Hartley Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taft%E2%80%93Hartley_Act)'s implementation.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994154-55] Reagan aligned the union with the studios against the Conference of Studio Unions in the aftermath of the [Hollywood Black Friday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Black_Friday) strike. In *The Invisible Bridge*, [Rick Perlstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Perlstein) wrote that Reagan's actions lent legitimacy to the studio's efforts to crush the more radical union by giving liberals in SAG who did not want to strike "a story that turned them into moral innocents instead of scabs".[^cite_note-56] On April 10, the [Federal Bureau of Investigation](11127.md) (FBI) interviewed Reagan and he provided the names of actors whom he believed to be [communist sympathizers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_sympathizers).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199832-57] During a [House Un-American Activities Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/House_Un-American_Activities_Committee) hearing, Reagan testified that some guild members were associated with the [Communist Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_USA)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200397-58] and that he was well-informed about a "jurisdictional strike".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200398-59] When asked if he was aware of communist efforts within the [Screen Writers Guild](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Screen_Writers_Guild), he called information about the efforts "hearsay".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201589-60] Reagan resigned as SAG president November 10, 1952, but remained on the board.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEEliot2008266-61]

The SAG fought with film producers for the right to receive [residual payments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residual_\(entertainment_industry\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199835-62] and on November 16, 1959, the board elected Reagan SAG president for the second time.[^cite_note-63] Reagan managed to secure payments for actors whose theatrical films had been released between 1948 and 1959 and subsequently televised. The producers were initially required to pay the actors fees, but they ultimately settled instead for providing pensions and paying residuals for films made after 1959. Reagan resigned from the SAG presidency on June 7, 1960, and also left the board.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003111–112-64]

### Marriages and children

Actors Jane Wyman and Ronald Reagan at a Los Angeles premiere for the 1942 film Tales of Manhattan

Reagan and Jane Wyman, 1942

The Reagans at The Stork Club in New York City, 1952

Ronald and Nancy Reagan, 1952

In January 1940, Reagan married [Jane Wyman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jane_Wyman), his co-star in the 1938 film *[Brother Rat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brother_Rat)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands201543-65][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201223-66] Together, they had two biological daughters: [Maureen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maureen_Reagan) in 1941,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201225-67] and Christine in 1947 (born prematurely and died the following day).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201229-68] They adopted one son, [Michael](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michael_Reagan), in 1945.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201227-49] Wyman filed for divorce in June 1948. She was uninterested in politics, and occasionally recriminated, reconciled, and [separated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marital_separation) with him. Although Reagan was unprepared,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201229-68] the divorce was finalized in July 1949.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200373–74-69] Later that year, Reagan met [Nancy Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nancy_Davis) after she contacted him in his capacity as the SAG president about her name appearing on a communist [blacklist in Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blacklist_in_Hollywood); she had been mistaken for another Nancy Davis.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015109-70] They married in March 1952,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015113-71] and had two children, [Patti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patti_Davis) in October 1952, and [Ron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ron_Reagan) in May 1958.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015199-72]

### Television

Reagan became the host of [MCA Inc.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MCA_Inc.) television production *[General Electric Theater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric_Theater)*[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEVaughn1994236-43] at Wasserman's recommendation. It featured multiple guest stars,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015120-73] and Ronald and Nancy Reagan, continuing to use her stage name Nancy Davis, acted together in three episodes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEMetzger198926-74] When asked how Reagan was able to recruit such stars to appear on the show during television's infancy, he replied, "Good stories, top direction, production quality".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015122-75] However, the viewership declined in the 1960s and the show was canceled in 1962.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015131–132-76] In 1965, Reagan became the host[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015145-77] of another MCA production, *[Death Valley Days](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Death_Valley_Days)*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199836-78]

## Early political activities

Reagan speaking for presidential candidate Barry Goldwater in Los Angeles, 1964

Reagan [campaigning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater_1964_presidential_campaign) with [Barry Goldwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater), 1964

Reagan began his political career as a [Democrat](5043544.md), viewing [Franklin D. Roosevelt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franklin_D._Roosevelt) as "a true hero".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEYager200612–13-79] He joined the [American Veterans Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Veterans_Committee_\(1943%E2%80%932008\)) and [Hollywood Independent Citizens Committee of the Arts, Sciences and Professions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Independent_Citizens_Committee_of_the_Arts,_Sciences_and_Professions) (HICCASP), worked with the [AFL–CIO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AFL%E2%80%93CIO) to fight [right-to-work laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right-to-work_law),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201228-80] and continued to speak out against racism when he was in Hollywood.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton1998139-81] In 1945, Reagan planned to lead an HICCASP anti-nuclear rally, but Warner Bros. prevented him from going.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTELettow20064–5-82] In 1946, he appeared in a radio program called *Operation Terror* to speak out against rising [Ku Klux Klan](16779.md) activity, calling it a "capably organized systematic campaign of fascist violence and intimidation and horror".[^cite_note-Racism-83] Reagan supported [Harry S. Truman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harry_S._Truman) in the [1948 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_United_States_presidential_election),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201249-84] and [Helen Gahagan Douglas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helen_Gahagan_Douglas) for the [US Senate in 1950](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1950_United_States_Senate_election_in_California). It was Reagan's belief that communism was a powerful backstage influence in Hollywood that led him to rally his friends against them.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201228-80]

Reagan began shifting to the right when he supported the presidential campaigns of [Dwight D. Eisenhower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dwight_D._Eisenhower) in 1952 and [Richard Nixon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Nixon) in 1960.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200053-85] When Reagan was contracted by [General Electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_Electric) (GE), he gave speeches to their employees. His speeches had a positive take on [free markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_market).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201242–43-86] In 1961, Reagan adapted his speeches into [another speech](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_Speaks_Out_Against_Socialized_Medicine) to criticize [Medicare](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medicare_\(United_States\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESkidmore2008103-87] In his view, its legislation would have meant "the end of [individual freedom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Individual_freedom) in the United States".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEOnge2017240-88] In 1962, Reagan was dropped by GE,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003112-89] and he formally registered as a [Republican](32070.md).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200053-85]

In the [1964 US presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_United_States_presidential_election), Reagan gave a speech for presidential contender [Barry Goldwater](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201255-90] that was eventually referred to as "[A Time for Choosing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Time_for_Choosing)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003132-91] Reagan argued that the [Founding Fathers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Founding_Fathers) "knew that governments don't control things. And they knew when a government sets out to do that, it must use force and coercion to achieve its purpose"[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReagan199027-92] and that "We've been told increasingly that we must choose between left or right".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReagan199099–100-93] Even though the speech was not enough to turn around the faltering [Goldwater campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Barry_Goldwater_1964_presidential_campaign), it increased Reagan's profile among conservatives. [David S. Broder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/David_S._Broder) and [Stephen H. Hess](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stephen_H._Hess) called it "the most successful national political debut since [William Jennings Bryan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Jennings_Bryan) electrified the [1896 Democratic convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1896_Democratic_National_Convention) with his famous ['Cross of Gold' address](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross_of_Gold_speech)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201255-90]

### 1966 California gubernatorial election

Further information: [1966 California gubernatorial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1966_California_gubernatorial_election)

The Reagans celebrating Ronald's victory in the 1966 California gubernatorial election at The Biltmore Hotel in Los Angeles

Ronald and Nancy Reagan celebrating his gubernatorial election victory, November 1966

In January 1966, Reagan announced his candidacy for the [California governorship](62517.md),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003141-94] repeating his stances on individual freedom and [big government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_government).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015148-95] When he met with black Republicans in March,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015149-96] he was criticized for opposing the [Civil Rights Act of 1964](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_Rights_Act_of_1964). Reagan responded that bigotry was not in his nature[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003142-97] and later argued that certain provisions of the act infringed upon the rights of property owners.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015150-98] After the [Supreme Court of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_California) ruled that the initiative that repealed the [Rumford Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rumford_Act) was unconstitutional in May, he voiced his support for the act's repeal,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003147-99] but later preferred amending it.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPutnam200627-100] In the Republican primary, Reagan defeated [George Christopher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_Christopher_\(mayor\)),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003147–148-101] a moderate Republican[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003135-102] who [William F. Buckley Jr.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_F._Buckley_Jr.) thought had painted Reagan as extreme.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015148-95]

Reagan's general election opponent, incumbent governor [Pat Brown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pat_Brown), attempted to label Reagan as an extremist.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199869-103] Reagan portrayed himself as a political outsider,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003149-104] and charged Brown as responsible for the [Watts riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watts_riots) and lenient on crime.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199869-103] In numerous speeches, Reagan "hit the Brown administration about high taxes, uncontrolled spending, the radicals at the [University of California, Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Berkeley), and the need for [accountability](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accountability) in government".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201259-105] Meanwhile, many in the press perceived Reagan as "monumentally ignorant of state issues", though [Lou Cannon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lou_Cannon) said that Reagan benefited from an appearance he and Brown made on *[Meet the Press](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Meet_the_Press)* in September.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003158–159-106] Reagan won the governorship with 58% of the vote compared to Brown's 42%.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201260-107]

## California governorship (1967–1975)

Main article: [Governorship of Ronald Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Governorship_of_Ronald_Reagan)

The Reagans at an airport, 1972

The Reagans in 1972

Brown had spent much of California's funds on new programs, prompting them to use [accrual accounting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accrual_accounting) to avoid raising taxes. Consequently, it generated a larger deficit,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon20035-108] and Reagan called for reduced government spending and tax hikes to [balance the budget](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Balance_the_budget).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201264-109] He worked with [Jesse M. Unruh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_M._Unruh) on securing tax increases and promising future property tax cuts. This caused some conservatives to accuse Reagan of betraying his principles.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015157–159-110] As a result, taxes on sales, banks, corporate profits, inheritances, liquor, and cigarettes jumped. [Kevin Starr](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kevin_Starr) states Reagan "gave Californians the biggest tax hike in their history—and got away with it".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPutnam200626-111] In [the 1970 gubernatorial election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1970_California_gubernatorial_election), Unruh used Reagan's tax policy against him, saying it disproportionally favored the wealthy. Reagan countered that he was still committed to reducing property taxes.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTESchuparra201547–48-112] By 1973, the budget had a surplus, which Reagan preferred "to give back to the people".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003370-113]

In 1967, Reagan reacted to the [Black Panther Party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Black_Panther_Party)'s strategy of [copwatching](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copwatch) by signing the [Mulford Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulford_Act)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHayesFortunatoHibbing2021819-114] to prohibit the public carrying of firearms. The act was California's most restrictive piece of [gun control legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_politics_in_the_United_States), with critics saying that it was "overreacting to the political activism of organizations such as the Black Panthers".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECarter2002493-115] The act marked the beginning of both modern legislation and [public attitude studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_opinion_on_gun_control_in_the_United_States) on gun control.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHayesFortunatoHibbing2021819-114] Reagan also signed the 1967 Therapeutic Abortion Act that allowed abortions in the cases of rape and incest when a doctor determined the birth would impair the physical or mental health of the mother. He later expressed regret over signing it, saying that he was unaware of the mental health provision. He believed that doctors were interpreting the provision loosely, resulting in more abortions.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003209–214-116]

After Reagan won the 1966 election, he and his advisors planned a run in the [1968 Republican presidential primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199876-117] He ran as an unofficial candidate to cut into Nixon's southern support and be a compromise candidate if there were to be a [brokered convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brokered_convention). He won California's delegates,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGould201092–93-118] but Nixon secured enough delegates for [the nomination](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1968_Republican_National_Convention).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEGould201096–97-119]

Reagan had previously been critical of former governor Brown and university administrators for tolerating student demonstrations in the city of [Berkeley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Berkeley,_California), making it a major theme in his campaigning.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003271-120] On February 5, 1969, Reagan declared a state of emergency in response to [ongoing protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free_Speech_Movement#1966%E2%80%931970) and violence at the University of California, Berkeley, and sent in the [California Highway Patrol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Highway_Patrol). In May 1969, these officers, along with local officers from Berkeley and Alameda county, [clashed with protestors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1969_People's_Park_protest) over a site known as the [People's Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Park_\(Berkeley\)).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003291–292-121][^cite_note-122] One student was shot and killed while many police officers and two reporters were injured. Reagan then commanded the [state National Guard troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_National_Guard) to occupy Berkeley for seventeen days to subdue the protesters, allowing other students to attend class safely. In February 1970, violent protests broke out near the [University of California, Santa Barbara](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Santa_Barbara), where he once again deployed the National Guard. On April 7, Reagan defended his policies regarding campus protests, saying, "If it takes a bloodbath, let's get it over with. No more appeasement".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003295-123]

During his victorious reelection campaign in 1970, Reagan, remaining critical of government, promised to prioritize [welfare reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_reform).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201273,_75-124] He was concerned that the programs were disincentivizing work and that the growing welfare rolls would lead to both an unbalanced budget and another big tax hike in 1972.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201275-125] At the same time, the [Federal Reserve](10819.md) increased interest rates to combat inflation, putting the American economy in [a mild recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recession_of_1969%E2%80%931970). Reagan worked with [Bob Moretti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Moretti) to tighten up the eligibility requirements so that the financially needy could continue receiving payments. This was only accomplished after Reagan softened his criticism of Nixon's [Family Assistance Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Family_Assistance_Plan). Nixon then lifted regulations to shepherd California's experiment.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015179–181-126] In 1976, the [Employment Development Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Employment_Development_Department) published a report suggesting that the experiment that ran from 1971 to 1974 was unsuccessful.[^cite_note-127]

Reagan declined to run for the governorship [in 1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_California_gubernatorial_election) and it was won by Pat Brown's son, [Jerry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerry_Brown).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2000754–755-128] Reagan's governorship, as professor Gary K. Clabaugh writes, saw public schools deteriorate due to his opposition to additional basic education funding.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEClabaugh2004257-129] As for higher education, journalist William Trombley believed that the budget cuts Reagan enacted damaged Berkeley's student-faculty ratio and research.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003296-130] The homicide rate doubled and armed robbery rates rose by even more during Reagan's eight years, even with the many laws Reagan signed to try toughening criminal sentencing and reforming the criminal justice system.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003388-131] Reagan strongly supported capital punishment, but his efforts to enforce it were thwarted by *[People v. Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_v._Anderson)* in 1972.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003223–224-132] According to his son, Michael, Reagan said that he regretted signing the Family Law Act that granted [no-fault divorces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No-fault_divorce).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEReagan201167-133]

### 1976 Republican primaries

Main articles: [Ronald Reagan 1976 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_1976_presidential_campaign) and [1976 Republican Party presidential primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries)

Reagan and Gerald Ford shaking hands on the podium after Reagan narrowly lost the nomination at the 1976 Republican National Convention

Reagan and Gerald Ford shaking hands on the podium after Reagan narrowly lost the nomination at the [1976 Republican National Convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Republican_National_Convention)

Insufficiently conservative to Reagan[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201278-134] and many other Republicans,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrimuth201645-135] President [Gerald Ford](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gerald_Ford) suffered from multiple political and economic woes. Ford, running for president, was disappointed to hear him also run.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201284–87-136] Reagan was strongly critical of *détente* and Ford's policy of *détente* with the Soviet Union.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200648-137] He repeated "A Time for Choosing" around the country[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015193–194-138] before announcing his campaign on November 20, 1975, when he discussed economic and social problems, and to a lesser extent, foreign affairs.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrimuth201647-139] Both candidates were determined to knock each other out early in the primaries,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWitcover1977433-140] but Reagan would devastatingly lose the first five primaries beginning with New Hampshire,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201289–90-141] where he popularized the [welfare queen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Welfare_queen) narrative about [Linda Taylor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Linda_Taylor), exaggerating her misuse of welfare benefits and igniting voter resentment for welfare reform,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoris2007612–613-142] but never overtly mentioning her name or race.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2000457-143]

In Florida, Reagan used [racially coded rhetoric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dog_whistle_politics) to undermine [Food Stamps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Food_Stamps), referring to recipients as "strapping young buck\[s]",[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrimuth201648-144][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaney_López20144-145] and attacked Ford for handing the [Panama Canal](24849.md) to Panama's government, while Ford implied that Reagan would [end Social Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Security_debate_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201289–90-141] Then, in Illinois, Reagan again criticized Ford's policy and his secretary of state, [Henry Kissinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Kissinger).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWitcover1977404-146] Losing the first five primaries prompted Reagan to desperately win North Carolina's by running a grassroots campaign and uniting with the [Jesse Helms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesse_Helms) political machine that viciously attacked Ford. Reagan won an upset victory, convincing party delegates that Ford's nomination was no longer guaranteed.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201291Primuth201648-147] Reagan won subsequent victories in Texas, Alabama, Georgia, and Indiana with his attacks on social programs, opposition to [forced busing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forced_busing) to achieve school [desegregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Desegregation_in_the_United_States),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPrimuth201649–50-148] and repeated criticisms of Ford and Kissinger's policies, including *détente*.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005104-149]

Reagan's concession speech at the 1976 convention

The result was a seesaw battle for the 1,130 delegates required for their party's nomination that neither would reach before the [Kansas City convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Republican_National_Convention)[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201292–93-150] in August[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoller2004345-151] and Ford replacing mentions of *détente* with Reagan's preferred phrase, "[peace through strength](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peace_through_strength)".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEKengor200649-152] Reagan took [John Sears](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Sears_\(political_strategist\))' advice of choosing liberal [Richard Schweiker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Richard_Schweiker) as his running mate, hoping to pry loose of delegates from Pennsylvania and other states, and distract Ford. Instead, conservatives were left alienated, and Ford picked up the remaining uncommitted delegates, earning 1,187 to Reagan's 1,070.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015204-153]

After giving his acceptance speech, Ford invited Reagan to address the convention. Reagan gave an eloquent and stirring speech that overshadowed Ford's own acceptance address, despite being little more than five minutes long. Some delegates later stated that they left the convention wondering if they had voted for the wrong candidate.[^cite_note-154] A contemporary media account stated that if a motion to reconsider the nomination had been in order, it might have passed.[^cite_note-155]

In 1977, Ford told Cannon that Reagan's primary challenge contributed to his own narrow loss to Democrat [Jimmy Carter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jimmy_Carter) in the [1976 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_United_States_presidential_election).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2003432,_434-156]

### 1980 election

Main article: [Ronald Reagan 1980 presidential campaign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ronald_Reagan_1980_presidential_campaign)

Further information: [1980 Republican Party presidential primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries) and [1980 United States presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_presidential_election)

Results for the 1980 United States presidential election

1980 electoral vote results. Reagan won 489–49.

Reagan emerged as a vocal critic of President Carter in 1977. The [Panama Canal Treaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panama_Canal_Treaty)'s signing, the [1979 oil crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1979_oil_crisis), and rise in the interest, [inflation and unemployment rates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Misery_index_\(economics\)) helped set up his 1980 presidential campaign,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard201299–101-157] which he announced on November 13, 1979[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199886-158] with an indictment of the federal government.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012102-159] His announcement stressed his fundamental principles of tax cuts to stimulate the economy and having both a [small government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Small_government) and a strong [national defense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_defense),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199886–87-160] since he believed the United States was behind the Soviet Union militarily.[^cite_note-Bowman_2004-161] Heading into 1980, his age became an issue among the press, and the United States was in [a severe recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Early_1980s_recession).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012102–103-162]

In the [primaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Republican_Party_presidential_primaries), Reagan unexpectedly lost the [Iowa caucus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iowa_caucus) to [George H. W. Bush](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/George_H._W._Bush). Three days before the [New Hampshire primary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Hampshire_primary), the Reagan and Bush campaigns agreed to a one-on-one debate sponsored by *[The Telegraph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Telegraph_\(Nashua,_New_Hampshire\))* at [Nashua, New Hampshire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashua,_New_Hampshire), but hours before the debate, the Reagan campaign invited other candidates including [Bob Dole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bob_Dole), [John B. Anderson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_B._Anderson), [Howard Baker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Howard_Baker) and [Phil Crane](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phil_Crane).[^cite_note-163] Debate moderator Jon Breen denied seats to the other candidates, asserting that *The Telegraph* would violate federal campaign contribution laws if it sponsored the debate and changed the ground rules hours before the debate.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBirkner1987283–289-164] As a result, the Reagan campaign agreed to pay for the debate. Reagan said that as he was funding the event, he could decide who would participate.[^cite_note-165] During the debate, when Breen was laying out the ground rules and attempting to ask the first question, Reagan interrupted in protest to make an introductory statement and wanted other candidates to be included before the debate began.[^cite_note-166] The moderator asked Bob Malloy, the volume operator, to mute Reagan's microphone. After Breen repeated his demand to Malloy, Reagan furiously replied, "I am paying for this microphone, Mr. Green! \[*[sic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sic)*]".[^cite_note-168][^cite_note-169] This turned out to be the turning point of the debate and the primary race.[^cite_note-170] Ultimately, the four additional candidates left, and the debate continued between Reagan and Bush. Reagan's polling numbers improved, and he won the New Hampshire primary by more than 39,000 votes.[^cite_note-171] Soon thereafter, Reagan's opponents began dropping out of the primaries, including Anderson, who left the party to become an independent candidate. Reagan easily captured the presidential nomination and chose Bush as his running mate at the [Detroit convention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_Republican_National_Convention) in July.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199887–89-172]

The general election pitted Reagan against Carter amid the multitude of domestic concerns and ongoing [Iran hostage crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Iran_hostage_crisis) that began on November 4, 1979.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPemberton199889–90Woodard2012101-173] Reagan's campaign worried that Carter would be able to secure the release of the American hostages in [Iran](14653.md) as part of the [October surprise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/October_surprise),[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012110-174] Carter "suggested that Reagan would wreck [Social Security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_Security_\(United_States\))" and portrayed him as a warmonger,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200183–84-175] and Anderson carried support [from liberal Republicans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rockefeller_Republican) dissatisfied with Reagan's conservatism.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012110-174][^cite_note-177] One of Reagan's key strengths was his appeal to the rising conservative movement. Though most conservative leaders espoused cutting taxes and budget deficits, many conservatives focused more closely on social issues like abortion and [gay rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005130–134-178] Evangelical Protestants became an increasingly important voting bloc, and they generally supported Reagan.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005135–141,_150-179] Reagan also won the backing of [Reagan Democrats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reagan_Democrat).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005131-180] Though he advocated socially conservative viewpoints, Reagan focused much of his campaign on attacks against [Carter's foreign policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_Jimmy_Carter_administration).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005145–146-181]

In August, Reagan gave a [speech at the Neshoba County Fair](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States'_rights_speech), stating his belief in [states' rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/States'_rights). Historians like [Joseph Crespino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Crespino) argue that the visit was designed to incite racial animus in white Southern voters,[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECrespino20211-182] and some also saw these actions as an extension of the [Southern strategy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_strategy) to garner white support for Republican candidates.[^cite_note-183] Allies and supporters of Reagan have said that this was his typical anti-big government rhetoric, without racial context or intent.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBennettLivingston2021279-184][^cite_note-185] In the [October 28 debate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_presidential_debates), Carter chided Reagan for being against national health insurance. Reagan replied, "[There you go again](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/There_you_go_again)", though the audience laughed and viewers found him more appealing.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBrands2015228–229-186] Reagan later asked the audience if they were better off than they were four years ago, slightly paraphrasing Roosevelt's words in 1934.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200183-187] In 1983, Reagan's campaign managers were revealed to having [obtained Carter's debate briefing book](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Debategate) before the debates.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEBoller2004368-188] On November 4, 1980, Reagan won in a decisive victory in the [Electoral College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Electoral_College) over Carter, carrying 44 states and receiving 489 electoral votes to Carter's 49 in six states and the District of Columbia. He won the popular vote by a narrower margin, receiving nearly 51 percent to Carter's 41 percent and Anderson's 7 percent. Republicans [won a majority of seats in the Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_Senate_elections) for the first time since 1952[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon200187-189] while Democrats [retained the House of Representatives](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1980_United_States_House_of_Representatives_elections).[^cite_note-FOOTNOTECannon2000755-190]

## Presidency (1981–1989)

Main article: [Presidency of Ronald Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidency_of_Ronald_Reagan)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of the Ronald Reagan presidency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_the_Ronald_Reagan_presidency).

Further information: [Domestic policy of the Ronald Reagan administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Domestic_policy_of_the_Ronald_Reagan_administration) and [Foreign policy of the Ronald Reagan administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_policy_of_the_Ronald_Reagan_administration)

### First inauguration

Main article: [First inauguration of Ronald Reagan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_inauguration_of_Ronald_Reagan)

Reagan speaking at the podium with dignitaries behind

Reagan delivers his inaugural address from the [US Capitol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/US_Capitol) (audio only).

Reagan was inaugurated as the 40th president of the United States on January 20, 1981.[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012xiv-191] [Chief Justice](31739.md) [Warren E. Burger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warren_E._Burger) administered the [presidential oath of office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oath_of_office_of_the_president_of_the_United_States).[^cite_note-JCCIC-192] In [his inaugural address](https://en.wikisource.org/wiki/en\:Ronald%20Reagan's%20First%20Inaugural%20Address), Reagan commented on the country's economic malaise, arguing, "In this present crisis, government is not the solution to our problem, government is the problem".[^cite_note-FOOTNOTEWoodard2012116–117-193] As a final insult to President Carter, Iran waited until Reagan had been sworn in before announcing the release of their American hostages.[^cite_note-194][^cite_note-FOOTNOTEPatterson2005126-195]
